OI-105FreeLLMAPI provisioning: the endpoint and the pinned models

One line

When the owner's FreeLLMAPI router is running, name its endpoint and the models to pin, and confirm it holds a one-model chain per pinned id, so the FreeLLMAPI route row can ship and take its one live call.

What is being decided

Moved out of OI-104 (WI-788's checkpoint, its question Q-4) at the owner's direction, 2026-10-04: "You can do whatever is desired to build up but the router isn't active yet so it will still be an open item to followup on when I have it".

WHAT IS HELD. WI-788's design note (chapter 2 section 5) ships a FreeLLMAPI route row only for a pinned model id whose chain, declared in the account home's FreeLLMAPI configuration, holds that one model, so the row's family is knowable; a router alias such as auto is never a row. The one live call the owner authorized (OI-104 Q-3) checks that the served model equals the pinned one. Neither can happen without a running endpoint: the 2026-10-04 probes verified the per-route OpenCode config (opencode models freellmapi lists freellmapi/auto), and a run fails at the connection to 127.0.0.1:3001.

WHAT IS NOT HELD. S788-routes builds everything that needs no endpoint: the FreeLLMAPI account template and OpenCode config, the Grok row, and the refusal of a FreeLLMAPI row with no declared one-model chain.

WHAT TO RULE, once the router runs: the endpoint (a local install at :3001, or hosted), the model ids to pin, and whether FreeLLMAPI can declare a one-model chain per pinned id.

Blast radius

Only the FreeLLMAPI route row and its live call (part of S788-routes' acceptance) wait on this; the rest of S788-routes and every other S788-* row proceed. Its queued placeholder WI-795 reads blocked.

Options
  • (a) PROVISION: name the endpoint and the pinned ids, confirming a one-model chain per id; the FreeLLMAPI row ships and its live call is recorded.
  • (b) PROVISION WITHOUT ONE-MODEL CHAINS: no FreeLLMAPI row ships (the note's rule), and the template stays for a later router.
  • (c) DROP FREELLMAPI: the template and config are retired and no row is planned.
Recommendation

Keep pending until the router is running, then (a) if FreeLLMAPI can declare a one-model chain per pinned id, else (b).

2 · Approval & re-attestation

SR-224A skill whose description cannot say when to use it fails the skills-index checkapproval owed

Requirement

If a skill's description is shorter than the declared description floor, then the kit's skills-index check shall fail, naming that skill.

Rationale

A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-005 asks that agents and humans work from the same playbook; it does not name what a skill's description must carry, so this obligation arrives through the first-run-adopter lens rather than through the need's text. An agent reads only a skill's description to decide whether to load it, so a description too short to say when to use the skill is shipped guidance an adopter's agent never reads: the skill works only for someone who already knows it exists, the undocumented-knowledge failure that lens listens for. A length floor is a lexical proxy, not a proof that a description is good: it catches the one-line stub that cannot state a trigger and leaves the wording to review. A reviewer's judgement of every description was the alternative, and it lost as the only guard because nothing would re-ask it when a skill is added. Fed back to the need: SN-005's acceptance could name the skills' descriptions as part of the playbook; until it does, this row is derived and says so.

SR SR-224Drafted — never approved

No cell differs from the approved snapshot. This row is here because its own Status asks for a human, not because its text moved.

Low: a new observation record supersedes this one; the rubric fix row (filed with this landing) triggers a re-judge through TC-055's declared inputs anyway.

Why not escalated

Findings are claims (owner, 2026-07-30), and the adjudicator's ruling on a disputed finding is final (OI-103 Q3); both refutations cite the render, not opinion.

D-004high riskdocs/decisions/wi-806.toml

Decided

The landing's squash is exempt at the hook by verification, not by trust. While git's own SQUASH_MSG names a squashed tip, the exemption holds only when HEAD is an ancestor of that tip (the lane was rebased onto trunk, as acts serialize and integrate's own trunk_is_ancestor already requires) AND the index's approval-act registries and record are byte-equal to the tip's own; then each commit HEAD..tip is judged against its parents, as the slot does, and the index is not. Otherwise the index is judged like any commit, and a refusal ends with a hint to rebase the lane onto trunk first. check.py reads SQUASH_MSG; acceptance_record takes the tip. Amended twice after Sol REVIEW-A: r1 (MAJOR) found the first build's intersection of the index's changes against HEAD and the tip let a SQUASH_MSG left by an abandoned squash exempt a different direct commit; r2 (BLOCKER) found the round-1 rule (equality to git merge-tree --write-tree of the tip into HEAD) admitted a squash that git combined, from a trunk and a lane each editing a different line of one multiline cell, into a THIRD cell value no judged commit carried. The merge-tree dependency (git 2.38+) is dropped, not kept beside this rule.

Alternative passed over

(a) No hook exemption, so a hand landing of an act-taking lane needs --no-verify; (b) exempt any commit while SQUASH_MSG exists; (c) also refuse a hand-typed commit whose spine and record are byte-identical to the lane tip's; (d) round 1's rule, equality to git's merge of the tip into HEAD, which needs git 2.38 and admits a combined third value; (e) require the landing to introduce no cell value beyond the two judged trees, a per-cell three-way comparison.

Reversal cost

Drop the squashed argument, _squash_lines, SQUASH_REBASE_HINT and the SQUASH_MSG read: about twenty lines and four tests.

Why not escalated

Q-8 exempts the landing squash because the guard held on every lane commit, and a coordinator's hand landing never meets the merge slot, so only re-judging those commits makes that premise true there. Requiring a tip that contains HEAD makes the premise exact rather than approximate: the squash of a refreshed lane is its tip's tree for these files, so every staged spine and record byte is a judged commit's, a combined value included (the refresh carries it as its own judged commit). (e) would rebuild a merge check beside git's; a lane must be rebased before its act anyway, so it adds nothing. RESIDUE, left deliberately and narrowed: after an abandoned squash whose tip contains HEAD, a direct commit whose approval-act registries and record are byte-identical to that TIP's own is still exempt. Every byte of it was committed and judged in the lane, and the window is one commit: git deletes SQUASH_MSG at the next commit, and once trunk moves the tip no longer contains HEAD. Nothing at commit time tells it apart from the squash itself (git keeps no other squash state); option (c) would need a provenance record, which the owner's 'enforce at the commit, no marker convention' rule rules out. Any other spine text or record content beside it is judged plainly. High-risk for the owner's look because it keys hook behaviour on SQUASH_MSG; the residue is Sol's retained MAJOR, for the adjudicator.

D-001high riskdocs/decisions/wi-818.toml

Decided

The overrule coupling runs inside the existing ruling sync: acceptance_record.ruling_sync_lines now returns the open-item lines plus overrule_sync_lines over the same diff, so the pre-commit hook's ruling-sync step and the merge slot's _ruling_sync_refusal judge it with no new step, rung or flag. check.py and integrate.py change in docstrings only.

Reversal cost

D-004high riskdocs/decisions/wi-818.toml

Decided

The citing work item must be a spec under docs/work/queued/ or docs/work/active/ that the SAME commit adds or changes, whose new text carries the token docs/decisions/<run>.toml#D-NNN (a digit never following the id). A row that already cited the entry and is left untouched, and an archived (terminal) row, do not satisfy the overrule.

D-005high riskdocs/decisions/wi-818.toml

Decided

The overrule sync fails CLOSED: a commit whose tree carries a decisions record (one the diff touches) that does not parse as TOML is refused, at the pre-commit ruling-sync step and per lane commit at the merge slot, naming the record and the parse error. A parent side that does not parse reads as overruling nothing, so the commit that repairs it owes the citing work for every overrule the repaired record shows.

Alternative passed over

Read an unparseable record as overruling nothing on either side (the round-1 build), so an overrule hidden behind a syntax error is owed by the commit that makes it readable; or refuse an unparseable parent side too, as the open-item ruling sync does.

Reversal cost

Low: newly_overruled raises on an unparseable after-side and _owed_overrules turns that into a refusal line; reversing is the two branches. A lane already carrying such a commit needs its history rewritten to land.

Why not escalated

The round-1 fail-open reading let an overrule plus a syntax error pass both admission points, and a later repair that dropped the verdict passed too (Sol, round-1 MAJOR 1); the owner's standing rule forbids degenerate paths. Refusing only the new side, not the parent, mirrors the open-item sync's unreadable-is-not-unchanged rule without stranding the repair of a record broken before this rule: the repair is judged as overruling everything it shows, which is the strict reading.

D-013high riskdocs/decisions/wi-818.toml

Decided

The citation delimiter '#' joins the run-name alphabet's excluded set, and record_path REFUSES it: a run name carrying '#' raises ValueError naming the run and the character, and the merge slot's decisions-record rung reads that as an absent record, refused when one is owed. The citation pattern's path class cannot contain '#', so the first '#' after docs/decisions/ ends the path, and since no record name carries '#' that one parse is also the right one. A branch named owner.toml#D-002 or owner#cleanup has no record, and the token docs/decisions/owner.toml#D-002 only ever cites owner.toml's D-002. (Corrected in round 5, dispute 1 ruling: round 4 mapped '#' to '-', so owner#cleanup silently wrote, and could be satisfied by, owner-cleanup's record.)

Alternative passed over

Map '#' to '-' (the round-4 build, rejected by the dispute 1 ruling: a lossy rewrite of a git-valid character folds two branch names into one record); escape run names injectively, which moves every slash-named record; or keep '#' in run names and anchor the citation reader to the last .toml#D-<digits> in a token.

Reversal cost

Low: record_path's refusal and the rung's ValueError arm. A branch carrying '#' must be renamed before its lane can merge under a recording dial, and an adopter's record already named with '#' cannot be cited until it is renamed. The alphabet test pins the set against git.

Why not escalated

Sol round 3 MAJOR 1 asks that the producer and the reader leave one parse, and the dispute 1 ruling (Sol round 4 MAJOR upheld) settles the shape: refuse '#' rather than map it, which fails closed, adds no degenerate path and moves no record of any adopter (every name without '#' keeps trunk's path). The refusal reaches only a branch a person named by hand, under a recording dial, at the merge slot: no lane name in this kit produces '#' (lanes are wi-<stem>, session tags are sanitized). It stays high-risk for the owner, as the ruling directs. The residue is stated, not fixed here: a/b and a-b, and a reused branch name, still share one record (the ruling's finding F1, the owner's call).

D-014high riskdocs/decisions/wi-818.toml

Decided

A path list is decoded losslessly, and a path that is not UTF-8 is refused where it would be read. git_paths reads git's bytes and decodes UTF-8 with surrogateescape, so a byte that is not UTF-8 survives as a lone surrogate and the path round-trips to git's exact bytes. git_show, the one blob reader, raises UnreadableBlob for such a path before asking git, naming it with the byte written as a hex escape, and ruling_sync_lines' existing handler makes that a refusal line (its message reworded to name both causes). So a decisions record in the diff, an open spec read when an overrule is owed, and a citing row or the registry in the open-item arm are refused when their path is not UTF-8, while a non-UTF-8 path the syncs never read (a source file, say) is listed and left alone.

Alternative passed over

Decode strictly in git_paths and refuse, by an exception or by answering None, whenever any listed path is not UTF-8 (the build prompt's sketch).

Reversal cost

Low: git_paths' decode and the guard at the head of git_show. Two tests pin the boundary: a non-UTF-8 record refused at the commit and the merge slot, and a non-UTF-8 unrelated path not refused.

Why not escalated

Both shapes fail closed on Sol's probe. A strict listing would refuse every commit touching any non-UTF-8 path anywhere in a repository (a path git accepts), at the pre-commit hook and the merge slot, for a rule that only reads decisions records and work-item specs; the lossless listing refuses exactly the paths the syncs read, at the one blob reader they all share. The guard precedes the git call because a lone surrogate on a Windows command line reaches git as another path, which git answers as absent: the original defect. Known edge: a commit deleting or renaming a non-UTF-8 decisions record is refused too, since its parent side cannot be read; this rule refuses the commit that adds such a record, so one can only predate it, and reading it would need a second, stdin-fed blob reader. The shape also keeps acceptance_record.py at 998 SLOC, where a new exception and handler would pass the 1000 threshold.

D-001high riskdocs/decisions/wi-821.toml

Decided

docs/stack.ini [dupes-census] is stamped 2/2/32, the census reading after the burn-down (5/5/52 before it), naming its two residual groups: agent_policy.human_approves / human_approves_spine, kept as deliberate (D-009), and check_trajectory.read_rows / gen_release_checklist.load_csv, which are NOT deliberate but deferred: the spec orders that pair to wait on WI-817, which retires the non-TOML carriers and may delete one or both readers.

Alternative passed over

Keep the stamp at 0/0/0 and accept a standing census WARN (2/2/32 over a 0/0/0 baseline) on every run until WI-817 removes or merges the CSV pair and the deliberate pair is merged or allow-listed; or merge the two held-status predicates now to shrink the residual to the deferred pair.

Reversal cost

Three numbers and a comment block in docs/stack.ini.

Why not escalated

Not settled by the builder: this goes to the independent adjudicator. The case against it: the Done-when asks for a census reading of '0/0/0, or the remaining deliberate groups only', and for docs/stack.ini [dupes-census] to be 're-stamped down by hand'; the baseline's own convention is downward-only. Measured against the committed stamp, 0/0/0 -> 2/2/32 is a raise, and the CSV pair is deferred rather than deliberate, so the reading is not 'the remaining deliberate groups only'. The case for it: the committed 0/0/0 was stale, below the 5/5/52 the census actually read before this row (the drift the research's section 7 found), so the burn-down itself moves the reading down 5/5/52 -> 2/2/32; a stamp below the reading WARNs on every run over two named, explained residuals, which is a warning nobody acts on. The census is warn-only either way (D-7), so the choice changes only whether that WARN stands until WI-817.

D-002high riskdocs/decisions/wi-838.toml

Decided

A mint's bookkeeping lands only while the route's record still carries that call's own lease (holder equal to the keep's, expired or not); any other holder, or no lease, or no record, returns "store moved on". Before WI-838 a mint refused only when another call held a live lease on a live (not retired) record. Round 1 of this work item widened that to a live lease on any record, which still let a late first mint replace a replacement that had completed and released its lease (Codex Sol's round-1 MAJOR, reproduced red). The own-lease rule closes both orderings: a first call overruns its lease, a second takes the expired lease over and mints, and the late first call never replaces that record, whether the second is still in flight or already bookkept.

Alternative passed over

Keep a liveness rule (refuse only while another call's lease is live), which lets the late first mint replace a completed replacement, generation 1 -> 2; or compare generations, which needs the keep to carry the record's state at the lease.

Reversal cost

One condition in session_keep._landing and two TC-329 tests.

Why not escalated

The spec's gap is exactly 'the second bookkeeping replaces the first session's record', and LLR-270 promises that a record another session replaced is left alone. Every mint takes its own lease in keep_for before it launches, so the rule changes no normal path: the first mint, the remint over a retired record, the same-artifact retire and the drained-session remint all land on their own lease (the existing session_keep, session_service and coordinator_adjudicate tests pass unchanged). Recorded high-risk because it changes older behaviour at the edges: a mint whose record was deleted, made unreadable, or retaken by another call while it ran now records nothing, where before it wrote a fresh record. Such a session is simply not retained, and the next call mints.

D-002high riskdocs/decisions/wi-841.toml

Decided

The hold points are (a) the loop's next BUILD dispatch, asked from worker_endstate before a build or rework session (a review session is never held, and an adjudication row is never held), returning EXIT_NEEDS_HUMAN with label HELD; (b) the merge slot, a rung in _merge_refusal after the close records; (c) a hand close, a new pre-commit step done-when-blessed running integrate.py done-when-hold on a staged commit that moves a spec out of docs/work/active/<branch>/ into complete/partial/cancelled. The claimed text is read at the lane's integration base in the lane (worker base / agent_common.default_base), at trunk HEAD in the merge slot, and for a hand close at default_base when committed on the lane's own branch, else at HEAD.

Alternative passed over

Hold in route_session or run_iteration (both over the complexity budget, 37 and 15), or hold every session including reviews; or read the claimed text at the claim commit everywhere.

Reversal cost

Three call sites, each one line into kitlib.done_when.lane_hold, plus one check.py step tuple.

Why not escalated

The spec names the two consumers (next build dispatch, close and merge); the build prompt names these code points. Recorded high-risk because a hand close committed on trunk after a squash of the lane reads trunk's (already edited) copy as the claim and so passes: that path relies on the lane-side close commit having been judged by the same hook, and on intake's safety net.

D-004high riskdocs/decisions/wi-841.toml

Decided

Every Done-when verdict outcome covers the text it names: CLARITY, BLESSED and SUCCESSOR all release the hold. A SUCCESSOR verdict drafts the unblessed scope in a ## Dispositions section of the verdict file, and the merge-time intake mints those drafts (a SUCCESSOR verdict without a draft refuses the mint); the lane's text stands, never reversed.

Alternative passed over

Let SUCCESSOR keep the lane held until the owner rules; or have the coordinator file the successor by hand.

Reversal cost

Drop SUCCESSOR from kitlib.done_when.OUTCOMES' covering set and remove intake._successor_drafts.

Why not escalated

The spec: a moved scope the adjudicator would not bless is drafted as a successor, never a reversal. A lane never mints (R1), so the merge mints, as it already does for an adjudication row's Dispositions. Recorded high-risk because a lane whose scope was narrowed can then close on the narrowed text, with the dropped scope carried only by the minted successor; any verdict file under docs/reviews/ is read, so like every adjudication verdict it is trusted as written by its session.

tests/test_adjudicate_brief.py's producer assertion (every routed brief is declared by a mint source) now exempts combined and asserts instead that every kind it composes is declared. Why not escalated

D-002high riskdocs/decisions/wi-842.toml

Decided

A hand-back is refused while the holder's OWN relaunch request (relaunch.json naming the calling session) is pending, telling it to end the session so its relaunched successor takes the lease; the lease and the request are left unchanged. A request left by a previous holder (one that requested a relaunch and was then released by the owner) does not block the current holder's hand-back and is left in place, untouched: the existing session_end path already refuses it as foreign (moving it to .refused and recording relaunch-refused) at a later holder's exit, and a later holder's own request-relaunch replaces it. An unreadable request file does not block either. (Round 2: Codex Sol round-1 MAJOR, a previous holder's request stranded the current holder's lease.)

Alternative passed over

(a) Let the hand-back free the lease whatever request is on file, which silently cancels the holder's own requested relaunch; (b) block on any request file (round 1), which strands the current holder's lease behind a crashed predecessor's request; (c) move a superseded request aside to .refused inside the hand-back, a second refusal path beside session_end's.

Reversal cost

One helper (_own_request_pending) and its check in hand_back, and two tests.

Why not escalated

The spec makes the hand-back the close-out's last act 'unless it requested a relaunch', so the holder's own request and its hand-back are exclusive; the coordinator's round-2 direction scopes the block to the current holder's request and asks for one code path for superseded requests, which leaving it to session_end keeps. Recorded high-risk because it is a refusal the spec does not name and it leaves a stale request file until a later exit or request replaces it.

D-001high riskdocs/decisions/wi-846.toml

Decided

Competing credentials (Sol round 1, MAJOR 1): for a token family the launch environment is composed, not overlaid. Settings-file sources (apiKeyHelper, settings env blocks, a managed gateway sign-in) are not handled, and the bound is stated in the IF-282 contract and the session-protocol text; apiKeySource from the init event is not recorded.

Why not escalated

A route's own declared competitor is a contradiction to refuse, not to strip silently. The settings-file residue has no pre-launch remedy short of dropping project settings the kit relies on. D-003high riskdocs/decisions/wi-880.toml

Decided

Sitting 001's third return (a [run] line's bare python3 is not the menu interpreter on Windows) is answered by narrowing, not by code: LLR-047 and SR-046's new clause state that bare python is exact on every platform and python3 only on POSIX, where a Windows interpreter's directory has no python3. The RESYNC entry and run_menu's docstring say a line meant for Windows declares python. TC-047 drives python3 on POSIX, the platforms the narrowed claim covers.

Alternative passed over

Reversal cost

Why not escalated

Correction (sitting 002, 2026-10-10): Windows' official Python install manager does provide a python3 on PATH, so a Windows-run line declaring python3 reaches that manager's default rather than the menu interpreter; the narrowing stands and SR-046 states the platform split outright.

D-001high riskdocs/decisions/wi-881.toml

Decided

A Windows session's tree kill is a job object, not taskkill: the child is created suspended, assigned to its own job, then resumed with ntdll's NtResumeProcess, and a deadline kill terminates the job. On this workstation taskkill /F [/T] /PID times out after about 61 s and kills nothing (the builder's probes, 2026-10-10), so before this change only proc.kill() worked and a grandchild survived every deadline kill. NtResumeProcess is undocumented but long-standing; it is used because Popen discards the child's primary-thread handle, and the documented route (a thread snapshot to find that thread) takes about 1.2 s here. A child that cannot be contained is killed before it runs and the session errors; there is no fallback to an uncontained child. Probed live through the new spawn: node spawning a child, codex --version and claude --version all ran inside the job.

Alternative passed over

Resume through the documented thread-snapshot route (OpenThread plus ResumeThread) at about 1.2 s per spawn, or kill the direct child and walk the process list (1.2 s, and it misses a grandchild whose parent has exited).

Reversal cost

Low: the resume call is one function (_contain_in_job); swapping it for the documented route changes no interface.

Why not escalated

The owner's rule is to fix the single point of failure without a fallback path; the job object does that, and the undocumented call is isolated in one function the full-lane review will judge.

D-002high riskdocs/decisions/wi-881.toml

Decided

Answer sitting 001's first return by bounding the claim, not strengthening the mechanism: SR-238 and LLR-026 promise that every process still in the session's process group (POSIX) or job (Windows) ends. The residual is recorded as a decided gap in SR-238's Rationale: a POSIX descendant that leaves the group (setsid or setpgid), or a process another service starts on the session's behalf outside the job (WMI, the task scheduler, an already-running daemon), is not ended.

Alternative passed over

Strengthen POSIX by tracking descendants instead of the group: a /proc walk on Linux plus a child subreaper, with no equivalent on macOS, or a cgroup, which needs privilege. Each is per-OS code for a descendant that detaches on purpose, and the Windows job has the analogous service-started residual anyway.

Reversal cost

Medium: a later row adds descendant tracking behind _kill_tree and widens SR-238's acceptance; no interface changes.

Why not escalated

The sitting returned it as the coordinator's call. Bounding keeps the claim true to the mechanism, and the residual is written into the row where a reader finds it.

D-004high riskdocs/decisions/wi-881.toml

Decided

Narrow review 002's F1 (a job member created between the handle snapshot and the terminate was never waited on; 12 of 12 trials) is answered by closing the job to new members before the snapshot: _kill_tree sets the job's active-process limit to 1, then snapshots, then terminates, so no process can join after the snapshot (the builder's measurements: 384 creations refused in the gap, 0 unsignalled at return; a refused creation runs no code and holds no inherited pipe). A live member the kill cannot open for SYNCHRONIZE (access denied, told apart from a gone PID by its error code) makes the kill raise after the terminate rather than be read as exited. The cost: a session whose descendants deny their own SYNCHRONIZE right errors at every deadline kill.

Alternative passed over

Re-snapshot after the terminate (not airtight: after a job terminate a member leaves the PID list before it is signalled, 100 of 100), or a completion port (its messages are documented as not guaranteed). For an unopenable member, grant SYNCHRONIZE through the owner's WRITE_DAC right, which rewrites another process's permissions.

Reversal cost

Low: inside _kill_tree's Windows half; no interface changes.

Why not escalated

It closes the reviewed class at its one owner without a fallback path; the unopenable-member cost is loud, and the DACL rewrite waits for a real case.

D-008docs/decisions/coordinator-2026-10-14.toml

Decided

WI-912's round-1 scope critique (S3, MAJOR, the act turn's re-runs) is adopted with one change to the remedy: each authorized act is judged at the lane's HEAD in its committed tree, taken when HEAD carries all of it and owed when it carries none of it; any other combination, such as a flip committed without its snapshot copy, is refused before the launch, naming the row, instead of resumed; and the act turn refuses a lane with uncommitted changes, so a flip left uncommitted by an interrupted turn is discarded by the coordinator, recorded, before the re-run lists the whole act.

Alternative passed over

The critic's remedy as written: a status-only partial act at HEAD is resumed, the brief listing the missing snapshot and its commit without a second flip.

Reversal cost

Low before the claim: amend WI-912's S3 Re-runs answer, Trust ruling and Done-when tests, and re-critique.

Why not escalated

Both forms fail closed; refusing serves a state the commit hook's integrity floor may already exclude without adding a resume path for it, and the reachable partial states (an uncommitted flip, or a combined act stopped between its two commits) are handled either way. The confirming critique judges it before the claim.

D-005docs/decisions/coordinator-2026-10-17.toml

Decided

Owner direction in session (2026-10-17c), asked whether the box was quiet for WI-931's landing reading: 'It may not be quite, but run it anyways, it sounds like the bar needs to be relaxed. So a landing no longer waits for a quiet box: its bar runs on the box as it is, on the staged squash, beside its parent's reading taken in the same window (D-004's pairing), and the landing commit records both. A breach whose reading stays close to its parent's (the tier running the same tests, the gap a few seconds, as D-004's pairs ran within 9 s) is load and does not hold the landing; a reading well above its parent's, or a tier that grew, is charged to the change as before, and every test must still pass. WI-931's landing is the first: the staged squash read 60.5 s (over by 0.5 s) beside its parent's 58.7 s, the two runs concurrent at below-normal priority, both 2623 passed and 2 skipped, against a quiet 46.7 s for the same tier at WI-928's landing. The owner keeps the budget as it is, and asked whether machine load can be fetched mechanically: 'the spirit of the smoke tests is just to prevent egregiously heavy / long repeatative tests, but we never know what other items will be running on the computer or what priority the smoke test has been given.' Told it can, with the stdlib only but not trivially (Windows through a job object's CPU accounting and GetSystemTimes over ctypes; Linux through /proc/stat and getrusage; macOS weakest), and offered two designs (scale the wall budget by the foreign load sampled during the run, or budget the tier's own CPU-seconds with a looser wall cap for tests that sleep or wait), the owner chose the CPU-time budget, filed as a queued row after the program's current batch. D-005's paired-reading procedure stays until that row lands. At filing the owner ruled the draft's three calls as recommended: Q1 (a), the wall-clock cap sits above the ordinary loaded readings on record (58.7 to 287 s), so only an extreme window or a tier that sleeps or hangs for minutes breaches; Q2 (a), one CPU-seconds value measured on this workstation is applied everywhere, CI included, the report naming the CPU count, and a breach on another box is a finding, not a re-stamp; Q3 (a), the Windows job-object code moves first into one shared kit module, in a behaviour-preserving commit, which the session kill, the root conftest and the budget step all import.

Why not escalated

Why not escalated

D-005docs/decisions/wi-803.toml

Decided

Excludes: grammar: 'Excludes: <ref>[; <ref>] <sep> <reason>', optionally list-marked or bold, anywhere in the plan, where <sep> is an em dash, an en dash, or a hyphen with whitespace before it. Several refs on one line share the reason. A line with no reason excludes nothing and is a finding; a ref that does not resolve is a finding.

Alternative passed over

Em dash only, as the design writes it; or one ref per line.

Reversal cost

One regex (EXCLUDES_SEP_RE).

Why not escalated

Planner output from either family mixes the three dashes; accepting all three keeps the grammar mechanical without rejecting honest exclusions on punctuation. The design's form (em dash) is the documented one.

The SR/TC diff's TC set is every TC whose verifies cell names one of the item's sr_refs, of any status, read from docs/test/test-cases.toml. An absent TC registry prints a note and leaves the TC diff empty (no finding); an item with no sr_refs has an empty diff.

Alternative passed over

Restrict to Approved TCs, or fail when the TC registry is absent.

Reversal cost

One filter in verifying_tcs, or one finding in _load_gate.

Why not escalated

Today every live TC is Approved, so a status filter would change nothing here; the absent-registry rule is the one the SR and IF checks already follow (absent means unvalidated, never everything unknown, IF-060's note behaviour).

D-002docs/decisions/wi-806.toml

Decided

Every cell counts, traced cells (Module, CodeSymbol, SN-Refs, ...) included: an act commit carrying a re-pointed traced cell is refused, and the re-point lands in the text commit before it.

Alternative passed over

Why not escalated

Risk 6 reads 'change no spine cell except Status'. The cost is one extra commit when a re-point and an act coincide; the snapshot copies traced cells byte for byte, so they are text the act blesses.

D-003docs/decisions/wi-806.toml

Decided

A merge commit (a lane's refresh, or a merge in progress at the hook) is judged by what NEITHER parent carried: a record path, cell or row counts only when it differs from every parent.

Alternative passed over

Judge every commit against its first parent only, as the ruling-sync rung does.

Reversal cost

Pass parents[:1] in commit_text_then_act_lines and drop MERGE_HEAD from the staged bases.

Why not escalated

First-parent only would refuse every lane whose refresh merges in trunk's own text commit and act commit together, which the rule exists to require, so it would block the two-commit form it mandates. The held-status rung already judges a merge this way (merge_status_moves); an evil merge's own changes are still caught.

D-005docs/decisions/wi-806.toml

Decided

A root commit, and the staged tree before the first commit, are not judged: there is no earlier commit the text could have been read in.

Alternative passed over

Judge a root commit against the empty tree, refusing a first commit that both adds rows and seeds the record.

Reversal cost

Use _empty_tree as the base in two places.

Why not escalated

The ruling-sync rule answers a root commit the same way; a first commit carrying a seeded record is a bootstrap shape the separation cannot improve.

D-007docs/decisions/wi-818.toml

It rewrites complete top-level assignments only, found by a scanner that tracks TOML string and bracket state, never a string's contents; a rewritten or dropped assignment keeps its trailing comment (a dropped one leaves the comment on its own line); it re-parses the result and refuses (ValueError, the record left untouched and named) unless only the verdict keys changed, comparing NaN as equal to NaN. --check reports without writing.

D-010docs/decisions/wi-818.toml

Decided

One run-name alphabet serves the record path and the citation reader: the characters a run name cannot keep are exactly what git refuses in any branch name, one character at a time (the ASCII control characters, space and DEL, and ~ ^ : ? * [ and backslash), matched as an explicit ASCII class, plus the two delimiters a branch may carry, '/' and '#' (D-013). record_path maps '/' to '-', exactly as on trunk, and raises ValueError for a run name carrying any other excluded character ('#' or one git refuses); no other character is rewritten. The citation pattern matches every character the alphabet keeps, so owner+cleanup, owner@cleanup and owner<U+00A0>cleanup records are cited as written. Against trunk's record_path, which mapped only '/', every path record_path returns is unchanged; a name carrying '#' now has no path. (Corrected in round 4, Sol round 3 MAJOR 2: the round-3 build matched Python's Unicode whitespace class, which git does not refuse, so owner<U+00A0>cleanup and owner-cleanup collided on one path. Corrected in round 5, dispute 1 ruling: round 4 mapped '#' to '-' as well, so owner#cleanup wrote owner-cleanup's file; it is refused instead.)

Alternative passed over

Normalize in record_path instead: map every character outside the word characters, '.' and '-' to '-', so owner+cleanup writes owner-cleanup.toml and the citation pattern keeps its round-2 class.

Reversal cost

Low: swap the rewrite in record_path and the class in the citation pattern; the alphabet test pins all three outcomes (mapped, refused, kept) against git. A lane already carrying a record named with + or @ would need it renamed.

Why not escalated

Sol's round-2 MAJOR 2 asks only that producer and reader share one alphabet; widening the reader keeps every record name a lane may already carry valid, where normalizing would move them, and it avoids two branches (owner+x, owner@x) colliding on one file.

The spine scan's own changed-path read (_spine_revs) is left as it is.

D-004docs/decisions/wi-822.toml

Decided

An explicit take is a CLI (coordinator_guard.py take) run from the coordinator session: it records the session from CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID and, when no --transcript is given, the holder's first hook call (not a subagent's) fills the transcript path in. A take of a free lease succeeds; a take of a lease held by another session refuses naming the release; a re-take by the holder changes nothing (it never unlatches).

Reversal cost

Why not escalated

D-005docs/decisions/wi-822.toml

Decided

The successor is identified by a random token: SessionEnd writes it into the lease before launching, the launcher passes it to the new session as PT_COORDINATOR_TAKE, and that session's SessionStart hook takes the lease only when the token matches. A mismatched token is recorded as take-refused.

Alternative passed over

Take the lease at the first SessionStart after a launch, whatever session it is.

Reversal cost

The token field and one comparison.

Why not escalated

The spec requires the lease to pass to exactly one successor; any other session starting in the repo at that moment must not take it.

D-006docs/decisions/wi-822.toml

Exit reasons are an allow-list of the documented SessionEnd reasons other than clear and resume: logout, prompt_input_exit, other. An undocumented reason never launches.

A deny-list of clear and resume, launching on any other reason.

Reversal cost

Why not escalated

The spec says 'an exit reason, never clear or resume'; an allow-list fails closed on a future reason that is not an exit.

D-007docs/decisions/wi-822.toml

Decided

Unknown occupancy never latches and never refuses on its own: the holder's claim proceeds when no valid usage is readable, and the reading is recorded as unknown with its reason. A reading above the declared window is flagged as a window mismatch and still latches (its percent is over any threshold).

Alternative passed over

Refuse claims while occupancy is unknown.

Reversal cost

One condition in latch_reading.

Why not escalated

Right after a compaction no usage exists until the next reply; refusing then would stop a coordinator at its lowest context. The spec requires unknown never to read as 0%, which holds: unknown is reported as unknown.

D-009docs/decisions/wi-822.toml

Decided

The relaunch launchers run claude with the prompt as one argument: on Windows the .cmd calls coordinator_guard.py exec-claude --prompt-file, so Python quotes the multi-line prompt; the POSIX .sh opens Terminal (macOS) or x-terminal-emulator (elsewhere) and execs claude with the prompt file's text. The SessionEnd handler starts the launcher with a new console (Windows) or a new session (POSIX).

Alternative passed over

Reversal cost

Two small launcher files.

Why not escalated

D-011docs/decisions/wi-822.toml

Decided

A detached launch counts as confirmed when the started launcher is still running after LAUNCH_GRACE_SECONDS (5 s) or has exited 0 within it; a non-zero exit within the grace, a missing launcher, or any failure from writing the prompt file to starting the process restores the request, removes the successor token and the prompt file, and is reported (Sol round 1, MAJOR 2). On Windows the launcher keeps the new console's own standard handles (no redirection); the POSIX launcher, which opens its own terminal, keeps its streams on the null device.

Alternative passed over

Treat process creation as the launch (the round-1 build), or wait for the successor's SessionStart take before releasing the request.

Reversal cost

One wait in launch_detached and its constant.

Why not escalated

Sol reproduced both strandings. A launcher that fails (missing claude, bad root) exits at once; one that works keeps running on Windows (claude runs inside it) or exits 0 having opened a terminal on POSIX. Waiting for the successor's take cannot work from SessionEnd, whose session is ending; the take stays the final confirmation, and an untaken lease is the owner's recorded release.

The reader keeps stopping at the boundary: the preserved messages are named by uuid in compactMetadata.preservedMessages and are not rewritten after it, so until the first post-compaction reply the reading is unknown, never their stale pre-compaction usage.

D-013docs/decisions/wi-822.toml

Decided

IF-276 (the out/coordinator/ runtime files), minted in Terra's round 2, is removed again by the coordinator: its only declared consumer was its own owner, which tests/test_seam_resolution.py forbids, and integrate.claim reaches the state only through the IF-271 call. Correction (in-lane adjudicator, verdict 001): the POSIX relaunch launcher reads the prompt file itself, so that crossing is declared with the launcher seams the verdict asks for, not as an out/coordinator/ file seam. D-014docs/decisions/wi-822.toml

Decided

session_end holds the store lock from the request's acquisition through the successor-token save, the prompt write, the launch and its confirmation, and restores under that same hold, so restoration never reacquires the lock (Sol round 3, MAJOR: a restoration that had to reacquire it raised StoreBusy when another handler held the lock past the ten-second wait, stranding a consumed request and a successor token). The token save joins the launch's try block, so every failure after acquisition takes the one restoration route. Cost: the lock is held for up to LAUNCH_GRACE_SECONDS (5 s) plus the launcher's start; every other guard entry point (a claim, a hook, the successor's SessionStart take) waits on it, and each waits up to ten seconds, so none is refused by the hold alone. The successor's SessionStart take on Windows, which can start inside the grace, is delayed until the hold ends rather than lost.

Alternative passed over

Make restoration lock-free: rename the consumed request back without the lock and leave the successor token to be cleared later; or retry the reacquire with a longer wait.

Reversal cost

Move the launch out of the with-block in session_end and give _restore its own lock again.

Why not escalated

The coordinator asked for one restoration route and named holding the lock as a candidate. A lock-free restoration still needs the lock to clear the successor token (a lease read-modify-write), so it would split restoration into a locked and an unlocked half; a longer retry only moves the threshold at which the request strands. The hold fits inside the waits every other entry point already has (5 s grace against a 10 s wait, and a 120 s stale-lock takeover), and SessionEnd is a once-per-session event.

Decided

The window function lives in agent_policy.py (blackout_at, with parse_blackout moved there) as the one reader of [policies] blackout, re-exported by agent_common; agent_common.blackout_wake is deleted and its callers and tests move to blackout_at.

Alternative passed over

Keep it in agent_common (over 1000 SLOC and baselined), or a new kitlib module, which could not read the dial through declared_policy's legacy dual-read without importing upward.

Reversal cost

Low: a function move; every caller names agent_common.blackout_at.

Why not escalated

Placement inside the spec's 'one window function'; agent_policy is the module that owns the declared-policy read, and the move shrinks agent_common (re-stamped down 24 SLOC).

D-003docs/decisions/wi-834.toml

Decided

The loop's admission stays at its existing pre-session wait (wait_out_blackout, now through blackout_at), and every loop launch also handles the service's refusal by waiting and retrying (session_service.through_blackout: the worker session, the interactive sitting, the recovery probe and the dual-plan round). The loop itself therefore never runs a wrap-up adjudication inside the window: its in-flight session finishes (ruling (d)) and the next one waits.

Alternative passed over

Delete wait_out_blackout and let only the service's refusal pause the loop, so a loop-route adjudication of an active claim could run inside the window.

Reversal cost

Low: one call in run_iteration.

Why not escalated

The spec states the loop already pauses this way and that the loop handles the refusal with its existing blackout_wait; the wrap-up exception is written for the coordinator's close-down (ruling (f)).

D-010docs/decisions/wi-834.toml

Decided

Inside an armed blackout window, a main session's Bash or PowerShell command line the hook cannot read (an unclosed quote, $( substitution, backtick substitution or PowerShell here-string) is DENIED, fail-closed, with a reason naming why and asking for the line to be rewritten; outside the window no line is read at all. An unterminated here-document is not unreadable: bash runs it to the end of input, so its body is skipped to the end.

Alternative passed over

Fail open: treat an unreadable line as naming no model CLI and allow it.

Reversal cost

Low: one except clause in coordinator_guard.launch_reason and its three test cases.

Why not escalated

Fail-open would be a second, degenerate reading path beside the one interpretation (the owner's no-fallback rule): a line the hook cannot read is a line it cannot supervise. The cost is small and bounded: an unclosed quote is a syntax error the shell refuses anyway, the denial applies only to the main session and only inside the window, and the reason tells the session what to rewrite. The heredoc and here-string forms the close-down itself uses (writing the handoff, a commit message) are read, not refused, and are pinned in ALLOWED.

D-011docs/decisions/wi-834.toml

Decided

The hook reads a command line through one module, kitlib/shell_line.py (segments), in the tool's dialect: POSIX quoting for the Bash tool and PowerShell's quoting (single quotes with doubled quotes, backtick escapes, here-strings, braces as boundaries) for the PowerShell tool. Command substitutions ($(...), POSIX backticks) are read as further commands; here-document and here-string bodies are data and are not read. The guard keeps only the command-word reading (assignments, grammar words, the timeout and env prefixes, env's split string read through the same module). model_clis reads cmd_template cells through the kit's one template reader (agent_session.split_cmd via agent_common), so a JSON-array template names its CLI.

Alternative passed over

stdlib shlex in punctuation-aware POSIX mode, inside coordinator_guard.py.

Reversal cost

Low: one pure module and its import in coordinator_guard.py.

Why not escalated

shlex's POSIX mode returns a quoted ';' as the same token as the operator ';' (measured: echo ";" claude lexes to ['echo', ';', 'claude']), so it cannot tell a quoted operator from a boundary, which is REVIEW-A F1's own case; it also has no PowerShell mode and does not skip here-document bodies. The lexer is a separate module because coordinator_guard.py would otherwise reach 1105 SLOC, past the 1000-SLOC module ratchet (tests/test_module_size_ratchet.py); it is 929 with the lexer outside it. The new module is tagged to SR-229 / LLR-300; LLR-300's module and code_symbol cells need it added (the spine author's, at the checkpoint).

D-017docs/decisions/wi-834.toml

Decided

Answering full-lane round 012 F1, the one interpreter is resolved by the run launcher: each run.* launcher (root and template) runs its candidates (its .venv interpreter, then exactly its dev-setup's own runtime search in the same order: root run.cmd = scripts/dev-setup.ps1's py, python, python3, py -3.13, py -3.12, py -3.11; root run.sh = scripts/dev-setup.sh's python3, python, python3.13, python3.12, python3.11; the templates = their dev-setup templates' py, python, python3 and python3, python; each dev-setup's list lives in one variable, $PyCandidates / PY_CANDIDATES, and no candidate was added, a parity test pinning the four pairs) with one probe that prints sys.executable only at 3.11+, takes the first answer as a concrete executable path, hands it to the readiness operation as -Python / --python (omitted when none answered), and runs the menu on exactly that path. The readiness operation's run tier checks only the handed interpreter (the floor, by running it) and runs the kit's readers on it; it keeps no candidate search of its own, so its exit 0 speaks for the interpreter the menu runs on. The direct and list forms use the same resolution and, with none, exit 1 with the step, without the readiness operation, a prompt or a pause. The launcher's runnability-only choice and its py -3 fall-through are deleted. Because the sets match, whenever a dev-setup check reports a runtime, run resolves one. A runtime installed during a bare run's offers is not the resolved one, so that run still ends with the step, which names what was checked and says to install Python 3.11+ or put an installed one first on PATH, then run again.

Alternative passed over

The readiness operation resolves and reports its interpreter for the launcher to exec (a result file or a second output stream), or the launcher re-resolves after the readiness operation returns.

Reversal cost

Low: the four run.cmd / run.sh launchers' resolution blocks (run.command delegates), the dev-setup candidate variables and the four dev-setup run tiers' handed-interpreter branch, plus their pins in tests/test_run_devsetup.py.

Why not escalated

The direct and list forms must refuse a sub-floor interpreter without running the readiness operation, so a launcher-side resolution is needed anyway; putting the only one there and handing it to the readiness step leaves a single choice. A readiness-side report would need a second channel out of PowerShell and sh (stdout carries the report and the prompts), and a launcher re-resolution after it would be a second choice. Resolving to sys.executable also removes the py-launcher shebang substitution D-008 measured. The trade-off (an install during the run needs a second run) is visible in the step text; on Windows a freshly installed interpreter is not on the running launcher's PATH anyway.

D-021docs/decisions/wi-834.toml

Decided

Building round 5 (D-019, D-020): (1) the shell reader's words are a str subclass, kitlib.shell_line.Word, carrying quoted_at (the offset where its first quoted piece starts), so the guard tells PowerShell's $r = 'claude' (a value) from $r = claude (a command) without a second reading; coordinator_guard._assigned skips a leading assignment's targets and operator, also when the operator is written against the target ($r=claude), and a right-hand side opening with a quoted string or a variable is no command. (2) The opt-in binds each guard hook by replacing the example command's leading interpreter word with the opt-in's own sys.executable, as a forward-slash path in double quotes (read alike by the POSIX shell and cmd that Claude Code runs a hook command through); a re-run replaces the guard's own groups in .claude/settings.local.json and keeps every other group, so a different interpreter rebinds rather than duplicates. (3) GUARD is built at import from sys.executable, the hook's own interpreter. (4) The relaunch launchers are this repository's own, so their candidates are root run.cmd's / run.sh's (scripts/dev-setup.ps1's and scripts/dev-setup.sh's runtime search after .venv), pinned by the same parity test.

Alternative passed over

Mark quotedness with a separate segments() return shape; append rather than replace guard groups on re-enable; pass the interpreter to the relaunch launchers from launch_command.

Reversal cost

Low: each is local to its function and pinned by its test.

Why not escalated

Construction inside D-019's and D-020's rulings; a Word subclass keeps every existing segments() caller unchanged, and the relaunch probe is the one the run launchers already use (D-019 names it).

D-022docs/decisions/wi-834.toml

Decided

Alternative passed over

Treat every variable right-hand side as data (missing the chained form); keep hooks_state's own group-membership test.

Why not escalated

D-011docs/decisions/wi-835.toml

Decided

The entry point refuses, with exit 2 and before anything launches or is written, a root that is not a lane: it must be the top of a linked worktree (git's worktree records through agent_common.worktree_records, whose first record is the primary checkout) on a branch whose committed tree still holds a claim under docs/work/active/<branch>/ (integrate.claimed_ids_on_branch, the integrator's own claim reader). A primary checkout, a detached HEAD, a branch with no claim and a directory outside any repository are each refused, naming why.

Alternative passed over

Refuse only the primary checkout (any linked worktree on any branch passes); or record the session log on trunk through a serialized, permitted trunk path.

Reversal cost

One function (lane_refusal) and its call in the entry point's input check.

Why not escalated

The owner's direction (D-008, confirmed) is that the entry point runs from the lane, and Sol's review showed a primary-checkout run commits its telemetry onto trunk, which integrate.audit rejects. A lane is defined by its claim, so the check reads the claim rather than trusting any branch name; both readers already exist, so no second primitive is written. integrate.py and this module share CMP-008, so the import adds no cross-component seam.

D-012docs/decisions/wi-835.toml

Decided

The entry point owns the verdict path the caller names exclusively: as its last check before launch it creates the path empty with O_CREAT|O_EXCL, so a path that exists, or that another call has already reserved, is refused (exit 2, 'name a fresh one'), and of two calls naming one absent path only the first launches. After the call, a non-zero exit or a timeout returns 1 without reading the file, and a reservation still empty (nothing written into it by this call's session) is a missing verdict (exit 1). A call refused before its launch (the sign-in refusal) removes its own still-empty reservation; a call that launched leaves the file, written or empty, for the coordinator to inspect.

Alternative passed over

Check only that the path does not exist (round 2, racy: a second call could write the verdict the first then reads); delete the reservation after every call; or write to an entry-point-chosen fresh path and report it.

Reversal cost

One function (reserve_verdict), one release on the pre-launch refusal, and one empty-file check in the report.

Why not escalated

Sol's round-2 review reproduced the race and the coordinator directed exclusive ownership rather than another existence check, keeping the path the caller names (the brief already embeds it, so the entry point cannot relocate the verdict without recomposing the brief). Leaving a launched call's file keeps the evidence of what that session did or did not write; a pre-launch refusal launched nothing, so its empty reservation carries no evidence and is removed so a retry may name the same path.

D-013docs/decisions/wi-835.toml

Decided

The sign-in probe reads only the documented answers: claude auth status is signed in only at exit 0 with output that is exactly one JSON object whose loggedIn is the boolean true, and missing only at exit 1 with loggedIn false; codex login status is read as a whole response: signed in only when, besides blank lines and codex's own PATH-alias warning (which 0.160.1 prints only for a home under the system temporary directory; a home under out/ gets none, probed), it is exactly one Logged in using <method> line at exit 0, and missing only when it is exactly Not logged in at exit 1. Everything else is unknown, which refuses, including a claude answer followed by any other text (a stderr warning included), a loggedIn of 1, and a codex answer beside a second answer or a fatal line (Sol round 2).

Alternative passed over

Extract the JSON object from surrounding text (round 1), or ignore the exit code for claude.

Reversal cost

Two small readers and a table in session_service.

Why not escalated

Sol's review showed the round-1 readers authorised undocumented answers; the coordinator directed the documented combinations only. A stray warning reading unknown refuses a launch that might have been fine, which is the safe direction under the no-fallback rule; the probe against this machine's signed-in ANTHROPIC home still reads signed-in.

D-001docs/decisions/wi-838.toml

Decided

The lease-only record is the record with only family, route_id and lease keys (session_keep.is_lease_only): no session_id, no state, no generation. A record counts as live, a session to resume, only when its state is active or draining, so a lease-only record, or a bare one with its lease stripped, mints. The readers were fixed (_hold's live test, load_honoured's tombstone step), not the writer.

Alternative passed over

Write a full placeholder record at the mint (state retired or a new minting state, an empty session_id, generation 0), so every reader keeps seeing every key.

Reversal cost

One predicate and the shape written in session_keep._hold; no stored record outlives a call, so no migration.

Why not escalated

The build prompt orders a lease-only record and readers fixed rather than the writer special-cased. A placeholder state would also show a session-less record as retired or as a new state in the record's state vocabulary (IF-247), which every state reader would have to learn.

D-020docs/decisions/wi-841.toml

Decided

Round 9 (Sol final gate, two MAJORs): a combined sitting's verdict grammar and its ONE validator live in kitlib.sitting (GRAMMAR, line_refusal, named_kinds, sitting_refusal, kind_part); adjudicate_brief's VERDICT_GRAMMAR entries for the sitting's kinds and its verdict check delegate there, and kitlib.done_when accepts a DONE-WHEN line only from a file kind_part finds valid (a single-kind Done-when verdict, or a combined sitting that passes the validator against the kinds it names, its line read from its own ## done-when section). A section heading is any kind-form ## <lower-case-token>, so an unrequested section refuses the sitting. Consequence: a sitting whose done-when section is missing, repeated, or whose DONE-WHEN line sits under another heading is now INVALID and covers nothing, so the merge's safety net mints the goalposts row rather than refusing the mint as ambiguous; only repeated Dispositions inside a valid done-when section still refuse.

Alternative passed over

Validate the sitting only at the coordinator's entry point (a rejected sitting's line then still blessed at the holds), or keep refusing the mint for those three shapes by reading invalid files.

Reversal cost

One validator module section and two delegations; the reader in done_when.

Why not escalated

SR-232 requires an invalid section to refuse the whole sitting with no other kind acting, and requires extra sections to refuse; reading an invalid file to refuse rather than mint would be a second path through a rejected verdict. Recorded because approved LLR-262's ambiguous-part clause narrows (listed for Terra).

D-021docs/decisions/wi-841.toml

Decided

Round 10 (Sol final2, one class - parse, don't validate): kitlib.sitting.parse(text, brief, kinds) is the ONE parser of a verdict file into {kind: (label, fields)}: a single-kind verdict has no kind-form section and exactly one complete machine line; a combined sitting names exactly the requested kinds in its one SITTING line, holds exactly one section per requested kind and none other, and each section holds exactly one complete own-kind line and no other kind's line. adjudicate_brief.verdict_refusal (for the sitting's kinds and the sitting) and kitlib.done_when._verdicts both consume it; the Done-when line is the parse's. The requested kinds are BOUND in a separate file beside the verdict, <verdict>.requested (brief = <class>, kinds = <a;b>), which coordinator_adjudicate creates exclusively at reservation (after reserve_verdict, before launch; a pre-launch refusal removes both) and commits after the call in its own telemetry commit. The covering reader parses each verdict against its binding; with none, a file with no kind-form section is a single-kind Done-when verdict and a combined one covers nothing.

Alternative passed over

Write the requested kinds INTO the reserved verdict file (lost when the adjudicator rewrites the file), encode them in the verdict's file name, or read them from the composed brief recorded in the session log (not in the tree the holds read).

Reversal cost

One parser module section, the binding file and its two writers (reserve, commit).

Why not escalated

The coordinator directed one parser for both consumers and a binding that survives the adjudicator rewriting the verdict; a separate file does, and failing closed (unbound combined covers nothing) keeps a missing binding from releasing a hold. Recorded high-risk-adjacent: the binding is trusted as written, like the verdict beside it.

D-022docs/decisions/wi-841.toml

Decided

Round 11 (Sol final3, three MAJORs of one class): acceptance is a RECORDED fact. The verdict's binding (<verdict>.requested) records outcome = pending|accepted|failed; kitlib.sitting.write_binding is its one writer (exclusive at reservation, pending) and adjudicate_brief.record_outcome is the one place a route records accepted (its own validation passed) or failed (a failed, timed-out or refused call, or an invalid verdict) and commits the binding. Both routes call it: coordinator_adjudicate after the call (from _report's result), and the loop's adjudication_bookkeeping after verdict_refusal. Every consumer reads verdicts through one reader, kitlib.sitting.accepted / accepted_at, which returns a parsed verdict only for an accepted, bound, valid file and None otherwise, never raising for a brief class it does not cover: the Done-when covering reader (and so dispatch, hand close, merge and the merge-time mint) and acceptance_record's held-rung re-attestation rung. LEGACY (no binding): a Done-when verdict covers nothing - DONE-WHEN verdicts are new in WI-841 and this lane's own Done-when is unchanged, so no legacy blessing exists to keep; a held-rung re-attestation naming an unbound verdict is refused at merge. Existing approvals stay readable: the rung judges only act-ledger entries a merge ADDS, so acts already on trunk (including those naming this lane's unbound verdicts 001-010) are never re-read; an in-flight act in an adopter repo naming a pre-upgrade unbound amendment verdict must be re-sat through a recording route (RESYNC says so).

Alternative passed over

Accept an unbound single-kind verdict as legacy when its text parses (keeps in-flight adopter acts mergeable, but reintroduces acceptance by inference), or record acceptance only on the coordinator route.

Reversal cost

The outcome field, one writer, one reader and four call sites.

Why not escalated

The coordinator directed acceptance as a recorded fact written only by the route that ran the call, one reader for every consumer, and a stated legacy rule; failing closed on unbound verdicts follows the owner's no-fallback rule. Recorded because it can refuse an in-flight adopter act at upgrade.

D-023docs/decisions/wi-841.toml

Decided

Round 12 (Sol final4) widens D-022's reach in two ways. (1) ONE acceptance rule for both routes: adjudicate_brief.record_outcome takes the CALL's success as an input (coordinator: exit 0 and not timed out; loop: exit 0 and classify_outcome neither ERROR nor TIMEOUT) and judges the verdict itself (verdict_refusal against the bound kinds), recording accepted only when both hold - so neither route can record a failed call's verdict accepted. (2) At merge, EVERY act the merge adds that names a verdict - a re-attestation on a held or a released rung, and so any first approval taken in the same act (the snapshot tool lets an act name a verdict only beside a re-attestation) - must name a verdict kitlib.sitting.accepted_at reads as accepted (kitlib.sitting.unaccepted_refusal, after the held-rung rung so its specific refusals read first). D-022's legacy rule therefore now applies to released-tier acts too: an added act naming an unbound verdict is refused. Acts already on trunk stay readable - only ledger entries a merge adds are judged (acceptance_record._added_acts, now also behind reattested_between) - and an act naming no verdict is judged by the existing rules alone.

Alternative passed over

Check acceptance only on held rungs (Sol's bypass), or require every adjudication act to name a verdict (would refuse released-tier acts that legitimately name none today).

Reversal cost

One argument on record_outcome and its two callers; one kitlib refusal and its rung.

Why not escalated

The coordinator directed one rule for both routes and acceptance for every act naming a verdict, with D-022's legacy rule applied to acts merging from now on and existing acts readable; acceptance_record.py stays under the threshold (993 counted lines) by consolidating its added-act readers.

D-024docs/decisions/wi-841.toml

Decided

Round 13 (Sol final5): a call's success has ONE derivation both routes share, session_service.call_succeeded(outcome): exit 0, no timeout, and no error result the CLI reported (is_error in its JSON result) - the real failure signals, never the displayed outcome (classify_outcome's COMMITTED can mask an error behind a commit) nor a commit's presence. Both routes hold the same session_service.Outcome: the coordinator passes its call's result; the loop's run_iteration records it on the session's plan (plan['call_ok']) for adjudication_bookkeeping, keeping session_bookkeeping's signature. The coordinator's _report uses the same test, so its narration matches what it records. record_outcome judges the verdict only when the call succeeded (call_ok and verdict_refusal(...)), so a failed call is recorded failed without its verdict being read (LLR-306's narration). A reservation refused because a binding already existed removes only this call's empty reservation (_release(path, bound=False)), never a binding another call owns.

Alternative passed over

Derive success separately in each route from code/timeout (Sol's bypass via is_error), or pass call_ok as a new session_bookkeeping argument (grows agent_loop past its ratchet for no gain).

Reversal cost

One function in session_service and its two callers; one plan key; one release flag.

Why not escalated

The coordinator directed one derivation from the real failure signals shared by both routes, ownership-aware cleanup, and a failed call recorded without reading its verdict; each is the smallest change that makes the stated rule true.

D-025docs/decisions/wi-841.toml

Decided

Round 14 (Sol final6 MAJOR): close the unnamed path by DERIVING each act's authority, not by requiring --verdict. At merge, every act an adjudication lane adds is tied to its adjudication's ACCEPTED verdict whether or not it names one: each kind the added acts take (a flip needs first-approval, a re-attestation amendment) must be covered by a verdict kitlib.sitting.accepted_at reads as accepted among the bindings the branch itself changed under docs/reviews (kitlib.sitting.branch_verdicts), a combined sitting covering each kind it requested (kitlib.sitting.uncovered_refusal, after the named-verdict check). A lane whose only verdict for a kind is failed, pending, invalid or unbound has no authority for that kind's act. A named verdict must still itself be accepted (D-023). Legacy: an adjudication lane carrying no binding (a pre-upgrade in-flight lane) is refused at merge and re-sat through a recording route, extending D-022's legacy rule to every adjudication act; acts already on trunk are never re-read.

Alternative passed over

Refuse any adjudication act that omits --verdict (the snapshot tool forbids --verdict without --reattests, so every first-approval-only act would need that tool, the act command in three brief templates and the composed act slot changed, an adopter-facing CLI change), or read the verdict path off the claimed row (the row carries none; the loop and the coordinator each choose it per call).

Reversal cost

One kitlib refusal and its rung; the test fixtures' branch bindings.

Why not escalated

The coordinator asked for the rule that leaves no unnamed path; deriving from the lane's own recorded bindings covers named and unnamed acts alike with no change to the act CLI or the templates, and the bindings are what both routes already commit in the lane.

D-026docs/decisions/wi-841.toml

Decided

A missing root or verdict path is owed without recording. Sol final7 MAJOR 1 (row-tied coverage) was NOT built: by the coordinators' standing rule a further crafted-input finding goes to the independent adjudicator as a named dispute, whose ruling sets the fix or files it as a follow-up.

D-027docs/decisions/wi-841.toml

Decided

D-029docs/decisions/wi-841.toml

Decided

Round 19 (Sol final10 MAJOR): the one parser reads machine lines strictly one PHYSICAL line at a time (kitlib.sitting._keyword_lines: a line whose first token is KEYWORD:, split into tokens; no regex spans a newline). Every such line counts, complete or not - a bare keyword, a label carried onto the next line, a label outside the enum or a missing field=value token makes the verdict invalid (_machine_lines / _machine_line / _incomplete). A single-kind verdict carries exactly one complete line of its kind and none of another sitting keyword; a combined sitting carries exactly one SITTING line and one complete line per requested kind across the WHOLE file (a keyword line outside every section included) and none of an unrequested kind (_keyword_count_refusal), checked after each section's own line so section-located messages stay. named_kinds and own_section use the same per-line reader. The MINOR: coordinator_adjudicate's Contract IF-285 (and IF-284) paragraphs now state the full success condition, session_service.call_succeeded.

Alternative passed over

Patch the regex so its whitespace class no longer spans a newline, and keep counting only matched lines (a bare keyword line would still go uncounted).

Reversal cost

Four small functions in kitlib.sitting replacing two regexes.

Why not escalated

The coordinator directed strict per-physical-line parsing in the one parser every reader uses, counting every keyword line complete or not.

D-003docs/decisions/wi-849.toml

Why not escalated

Sol r3 asked for one parser; the narrowest one is the one shape the writers produce.

D-003docs/decisions/wi-881.toml

Decided

Answer sitting 001's second return by waiting, not softening: after the kill, _kill_tree returns only once the group (POSIX) or job (Windows, its active-process count) is empty, and raises if it is not empty within one stated bound of 10 s. SR-238's 'before the coordinator continues' then holds as written, the test asserts the grandchild is gone the moment run_session returns, with no grace period, and the stated bound is the overrun after which the coordinator stops with an error instead of continuing. Built (round 2): on Windows the job's active-process count reads zero a few milliseconds before a terminated process is signalled (the builder's measurement, 2026-10-10), so the wait also holds a SYNCHRONIZE handle on each job member taken just before the terminate and waits until each is signalled; the count alone left the interruption test red. Residual, recorded with D-002's: on POSIX a zombie still counts as a group member, so under a PID 1 that never reaps (a container run without an init) every deadline kill raises after the bound. That failure is loud, not silent; RESYNC_PACK tells adopters to run under a reaping init.

Alternative passed over

Keep the kill asynchronous and state a grace bound in SR-238 (for example, descendants end within 5 s of the coordinator continuing), asserting that bound in the test.

Reversal cost

Low: the wait is the tail of one function.

Why not escalated

The sitting offered both routes; waiting makes the stated threshold the code's behaviour rather than a tolerance.

Alternative passed over

Leave it for a later row.

Reversal cost

D-006docs/decisions/wi-881.toml

Decided

Answering full-lane gate 006's F1, the spawn boundary owns the child (and its job) until it returns it, and one try/finally in run_session owns it from there, ending the tree on any exit but a natural one; the waiter's own interrupt handler is retired into that owner. Not built: an interruption that lands inside the standard library's process creation, before the child object reaches the kit's code (POSIX: during Popen's exec handshake, a running child is lost; Windows: at Popen's return, the child is left suspended and never runs). It is recorded as a residual in SR-238's Rationale.

Alternative passed over

Defer SIGINT around the launch (signal.pthread_sigmask on POSIX, a deferred handler on Windows), which works on the main thread only and adds signal plumbing to every launch.

Reversal cost

Low: a later row can add the deferral inside _spawn_session.

Why not escalated

The window is the stdlib's own, a few instructions wide, and on Windows the left-behind child never runs; recording it keeps SR-238 true without a main-thread-only mechanism.

D-007docs/decisions/wi-881.toml

Decided

Answering narrow review 007's F1, a failure to end the session's tree raises agent_session.TreeNotEnded, a BaseException no except OSError or except Exception catches, so run_session's launch-error conversion can no longer turn it into a session result. When the cleanup fails while an interrupt is in flight, TreeNotEnded wins (it names what the operator must act on) and the interrupt stays in its chain. One sibling site outside this row's surface is left for its own row: session_service.KeepWarm._ping swallows every exception on its background thread, so a keep-warm session whose tree could not be ended is recorded and dropped. Filed as a follow-up row on trunk at this session's close.

Alternative passed over

Subclass OSError and audit every handler, or widen this lane into session_service to carry the error from the keep-warm thread to the dispatcher.

Reversal cost

Low: one exception class; callers that need cleanup catch BaseException and re-raise, as RESYNC_PACK tells adopters.

Why not escalated

It makes the failure unrepresentable as a result at its one owner; the keep-warm site follows the stop-and-file rule for work outside the spec's surface.

Fail-closed corner, a coverage-extension note: a shipped change that adds one entry while removing a landed one counts nothing and is refused; it would take two changes. A pack-only change that re-anchors a landed entry still passes, as deliverable 1 leaves edits unjudged. The plan's text describing the rule as first built stays as the accepted plan of record; the fix is inside P1's surface.

D-006high riskdocs/decisions/coordinator-2026-10-14.toml

Decided

WI-911's round-2 scope critique (S2, MAJOR) is adopted: a review brief's convergence marking lets a non-convergence-class MAJOR pass to a successor row instead of holding the landing, so the row gains a Trust section. The marking is a construction, not a convention: only review_brief.py review renders it, from a new --stance choice (ordinary by default, or convergence) as one fixed ROUND FACTS line, refused on a narrow round; a hand-appended line is not a marking and the unattended loop marks none; the reviewer applies the reduced blocking rule only on that rendered line, and the coordinator lets a successor-marked finding leave the landing's path only for a round its lane decisions record shows as one of the three convergence rounds at the verdict's reviewed tip. Every failure leaves the stricter ordinary stance.

Alternative passed over

A coordinator-appended stance line named in the recipes, with the Trust section stating its failure rules (no code change); or reviewers that never relax, grading every round alike, with the coordinator alone applying the convergence blocking rule from the round's position.

Reversal cost

Low before the claim: amend WI-911's deliverables 3 and 7 and its Trust section, and re-critique.

Why not escalated

The critique's remedy asked for a Trust section, which the coordinator writes before the claim (coordinator-cycle section 1); the rendered choice reuses review_brief.py's existing round model, which already refuses a narrow round's shape in a full one. The Trust ruling itself goes to the owner to confirm or overrule, as every row's does.

Owner note

Owner, 2026-10-14, in session (the attended coordinator's question on WI-911's round-3 critique): only the dispute sitting may move a BLOCKER or MAJOR to a successor row. The reviewer only tags each finding's class, every open BLOCKER or MAJOR after a convergence round goes to the sitting, and the dispute grammar gains a SUCCESSOR ruling (WI-911 deliverable 8). The convergence marking carries no authority, so WI-911 loses the Trust section this entry chose.

Acted on by
WI-911 (complete)
